```json
{
  "abstract_verbatim": "Critical self-avoiding walks between prescribed, macroscopically separated boundary ports of a regular honeycomb hexagon have length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$ in probability, where <i>R</i> is the scale of the hexagon. We prove the corresponding statements for half-plane arches, parallel cuts and nonparallel pure cuts. The half-plane law also has mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$. A separate strip argument gives endpoint mean laws on one density-one set of heights and in an aligned, critically weighted mixture of all even heights in a macroscopic interval.",
```

<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> Critical self-avoiding walks between prescribed, macroscopically separated boundary ports of a regular honeycomb hexagon have length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$ in probability, where <i>R</i> is the scale of the hexagon. We prove the corresponding statements for half-plane arches, parallel cuts and nonparallel pure cuts. The half-plane law also has mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$. A separate strip argument gives endpoint mean laws on one density-one set of heights and in an aligned, critically weighted mixture of all even heights in a macroscopic interval.

- **claim-prescribed-endpoints** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-main)): Let $D_R$ be convex pure lattice hexagons which, after scaling by $R$, converge to a regular hexagon. Let $a_R,b_R$ be boundary ports whose scaled limits are distinct points in the relative interiors of sides. Then, for every $\varepsilon>0$, the critical chord law satisfies $$\mathbb P_{D_R;a_R,b_R} \{R^{4/3-\varepsilon}\le L(\gamma)\le R^{4/3+\varepsilon}\} \longrightarrow1.$$ The same conclusion holds in a full half-plane for prescribed boundary ports at distance $R$, in a strip of height comparable to $R$ for one prescribed port on each of the two opposite boundary cuts, at mutual distance $O(R)$, and in a wedge of angle $\pi/3$ or $2\pi/3$ formed by two nonparallel pure cuts, with one prescribed port on each ray. In the wedge, each endpoint has clearance comparable to $R$ from the other cut and distance $O(R)$ from the apex. Constants are uniform on fixed compact sets of these nondegenerate scaled geometries. In each case the normalizing mass is at least $cR^{-5/4}$.
  - Explanation: For convex pure hexagons approaching a regular hexagon, a critical walk between two prescribed ports in side interiors has length of order scale to the power four thirds in probability. The same holds in a half-plane, a strip with one port on each cut, and a wedge of two nonparallel pure cuts, with normalizing mass bounded below.
- **claim-finite-inputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-finite)): The following estimates hold in the port convention above.* 1. *For every integer $h\ge0$, $B_h\asymp(1+h)^{-1/4}$. For all sufficiently large integers $h$, the length-weighted bridge mass is at most $Ch^{13/12}$, and $B_h\{L\ge ch^{4/3}\}\ge ch^{-1/4}$. For every integer $h\ge1$ and every $r\ge0$, the mass of bridges of diameter exceeding $r$ is at most $C(1+r)^{-1/4}$.* 2. *In a convex pure domain, the mass from one boundary source of completed paths of diameter at least $r$ is at most $C(1+r)^{-1/4}$ for $r\ge0$. For the half-plane arch kernel and integer $l\ge1$, $K_l\asymp l^{-5/4}$ and $K_{l+1}\le K_l$. The same monotonicity comparison is valid along one straight side of a convex pure truncation whenever both compared adjacent ports remain on that side. Arches of diameter at most $Al$ retain mass $cl^{-5/4}$ for a fixed $A$.* 3. *From a prescribed port $a$ with inward pure normal $n$, consider the terminal line $m\cdot(z-a)=s$, where $m$ is its outward pure unit normal and the angle between $n$ and $m$ is at most $\pi/3$. Here $s>0$ is the Euclidean distance from $a$ to the terminal line. Crossings that stay in the initial half-plane and before the terminal line until their final exit have mass comparable to $s^{-1/4}$. For any fixed positive relative tolerance, and all sufficiently large admissible $s$ with a threshold depending only on that tolerance, the lower bound can be confined within distance equal to that tolerance times $s$ of the segment $a+[0,s]m$. The corresponding two-cut upper bound holds without confinement.* 4. *In a convex pure domain of diameter $O(R)$, the total length-weighted mass of chords, summed over ordered boundary endpoint pairs, is at most $R^{25/12+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Collects finite estimates taken from a companion work: strip bridge mass decays like a power, length-weighted bridge mass and the arch kernel have matching power bounds, crossing mass to a terminal line is controlled, and total length-weighted chord mass in a convex domain is bounded.
- **claim-renewal-inputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-renewal)): The mass $p$ is a probability. Successive complete pieces have independent law $p$, and, for $s>0$, $$\sum_{h\ge0}B_he^{-sh}= (1-\mathbb E_p e^{-sH})^{-1}\asymp s^{-3/4}\quad(s\downarrow0).$$ Moreover, $$p(H>x)+p(D>x)\le Cx^{-3/4},\quad \mathbb E_p[L;H\le x]\le x^{7/12+o(1)},$$ $$p(L>n)\le n^{-9/16+o(1)},\qquad 1-\mathbb E_p e^{-uL}=u^{9/16+o(1)}.$$ Almost surely, for independent pieces, their first $k$ heights and lengths sum respectively to $k^{4/3+o(1)}$ and $k^{16/9+o(1)}$, and their diameters sum to at most $k^{4/3+o(1)}$.
  - Explanation: Irreducible pieces carry a probability law, successive pieces are independent, and height, diameter and length tails together with the Laplace transforms of the bridge masses have explicit power behavior.
- **claim-interior-connectors** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-connectors)): For two macroscopically separated side-interior ports in the hexagons of Theorem <a href="#c:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:main">1</a>, the critical mass of connecting paths inside the hexagon is at least $cR^{-5/4}$. The same bound holds in a parallel strip of thickness comparable to $R$, with one port on each opposite cut and displacement $O(R)$, and in compact nondegenerate wedge geometries with one port on each ray, as in Theorem <a href="#c:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:main">1</a>. The constants are uniform on fixed compact sets of these scaled configurations.
  - Explanation: Between two separated side-interior ports the critical mass of connecting paths inside the domain is bounded below by a power of the scale, via a cut-averaging construction with second-moment control; this supplies the normalizer for the prescribed-endpoint laws.
- **claim-window-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-window-law)): In the hexagons of Theorem <a href="#c:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:main">1</a>, fix a source port tending after scaling to a side-interior point. Let the terminal ports form a macroscopic interval converging to a compact side-interior interval at positive distance from the source limit. Under critical weights, normalized over those chords, length is $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ in probability. The same holds when the source is summed over a separated macroscopic side-interior interval. For each fixed source the normalizer is comparable to $R^{-1/4}$.
  - Explanation: With the source fixed and the terminal port summed over a macroscopic interval, the walk length is of order scale to the power four thirds in probability, and the normalizer is of fixed power order.
- **claim-fixed-height-window** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-fixed-height-window)): Under critical weights on strict bridges of integral height $h$, with one initial port fixed and the terminal port summed, for every $\delta>0$, $$\Pr\{h^{4/3-\delta}\le L\le h^{4/3+\delta}\}\longrightarrow1.$$ The same conclusion holds after any restriction retaining a fixed positive fraction of $B_h$.
  - Explanation: For strict bridges of a given height with free terminal port, the length lies in a window around height to the four thirds with probability tending to one, even after restriction to a fixed fraction of the mass.
- **claim-positive-projection** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-positive-projection)): Let $n,m$ be pure unit normals with $m\ne-n$. There are constants $b,B,A,c_0>0$ such that, for every fixed $\delta>0$ and all sufficiently large integral $h$, the mass of strict $n$-bridges from a fixed port with endpoint displacement $v$, restricted by $$bh\le m\cdot v\le Bh,\qquad D\le Ah,\qquad h^{4/3-\delta}\le L\le h^{4/3+\delta},$$ is at least $c_0h^{-1/4}$. The constants may depend on $n,m$ but not on $\delta$; the threshold may depend on $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Bridges with positive displacement projection onto two non-opposite normals, bounded diameter and typical length still carry a fixed fraction of the bridge mass.
- **claim-mixed-displacement** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-mixed-displacement)): For every fixed $C_0<\infty$ there is $C<\infty$ such that, for $s\ge1$, $$\label{c:mixed-displacement-bound} \sum_{\|z\|\le C_0s}D_n(z)(1+\|m\cdot z\|)^{-1/4}\le Cs^{1/2}.$$ If $Y$ is the $m$-projection of one $n$-directed irreducible displacement, then its independent sums also satisfy $$\label{c:projected-concentration} \sup_y\Pr\Bigl\{\sum_{j\le k}Y_j=y\Bigr\}\le Ck^{-4/3}, \qquad \sup_w\mathbb E\Bigl(1+\Bigl\|w+\sum_{j\le k}Y_j\Bigr\|\Bigr)^{-1/4} \le Ck^{-1/3}.$$
  - Explanation: A weighted sum of translation costs over displacements in a ball is bounded by a square-root power of the radius, and sums of projected displacements satisfy atom and negative-moment bounds.
- **claim-attachment-moments** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-attachment-proposition)): Let $\mathcal E$ be an event of input chords from $a$ to the stated terminal interval, of critical mass $E$. The fixed-source attachment construction just described has a translation count $N_s$ on output chords and critical output measure $\mu_a$ such that $$\label{c:attachment-moments} \int N_s\,d\mu_a\ge cs^{1/2}E, \qquad \int N_s^2\,d\mu_a\le CsR^{-1/4}.$$ Here the side-1 outer landing is exactly $a$; the side-2 outer landing is free on its support. The estimate applies also to input chords in the full two-cut domain, without a diameter bound.* *If the input chords lie in a convex pure domain of diameter $O(R)$, there is also a construction at scale $s=cR$ with both outer landings free, all outputs in one convex pure domain of diameter $O(R)$, and $$\label{c:free-attachment-moments} \int N_R^{\rm free}\,d\mu_{\rm free}\ge cR^{3/2}E, \qquad \int(N_R^{\rm free})^2\,d\mu_{\rm free}\le CR^{7/4}.$$ The measure $\mu_{\rm free}$ sums critical weights over ordered output endpoint pairs. In both constructions output length is the input length plus the two attached lengths. Constants are uniform for fixed compact endpoint geometries and independent of the fixed length slack $\delta$; the required lower threshold for $s$ may depend on $\delta$.
  - Explanation: Attaching translated bridges to an event of input chords yields first and second moment bounds for a translation count, in both a fixed-source version and a version with free outer landings.
- **claim-same-side-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-same-side-law)): Under the critical half-plane law $w(\gamma)/K_l$, for every $\varepsilon>0$, $$\mathbb P\{l^{4/3-\varepsilon}\le L(\gamma) \le l^{4/3+\varepsilon}\}\longrightarrow1.$$ The same conclusion holds in the hexagons of Theorem <a href="#c:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:main">1</a> when both scaled endpoints converge to distinct points in one side interior.
  - Explanation: For two ports on one boundary line, the half-plane arch has length of order separation to the four thirds in probability, and this transfers to hexagons when both endpoints lie in one side interior.
- **claim-pair-block** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-pair-block-estimates)): The pair-block mass $r$ is a probability. For $x\ge1$, $$r(T>x)+r(D_*>x)\le Cx^{-1/2}.$$ If $Y^{(m)}$ is the sum of the lateral differences of $m$ independent pair blocks, then $$\sup_j\Pr(Y^{(m)}=j)\le Cm^{-2}\qquad(m\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: Common renewal blocks of two independent sequences form a probability law with half-power tails, and sums of their lateral differences have small atoms.
- **claim-arch-kernel-regularity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-arch-kernel-regularity)): For positive integers $l,l'$ with $l\to\infty$ and $l'/l\to1$, $$K_{l'}/K_l\longrightarrow1.$$
  - Explanation: The half-plane arch kernel is asymptotically insensitive to sublinear changes of the gap: the ratio of neighboring values tends to one.
- **claim-strip-smoothness** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-strip-smoothness)): Let $B_N$ be the critical mass of strict bridges from one fixed port to all ports on the opposite pure cut at integral height $N$. With $\Delta f_N=f_{N+1}-f_N$, for every fixed integer $\ell\ge0$ and every $\eta>0$ there is $C_{\ell,\eta}<\infty$ such that $$\|\Delta^\ell B_N\|\le C_{\ell,\eta}N^{-1/4-\ell+\eta} \qquad(N\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: Every fixed forward difference of the strip bridge mass gains a full power of the width on top of the base decay, up to an arbitrarily small loss.
- **claim-model-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-model-kernel)): On every compact subset of the closed bidisk on which $\|1-v^2\overline u^2\|>0$, the kernel has continuous boundary values and $$\|\Delta^\ell K_N^0(v,u)\|\le C_\ell N^{1-\ell},\qquad \frac{K_N^0(v,u)}N\longrightarrow \frac{O(v)\overline{O(u)}}{1-v^2\overline u^2}.$$ Here $O$ is even, holomorphic and nonvanishing on a neighborhood of the closed disk, and positive on both the real and imaginary diameters.
  - Explanation: Away from the even diagonal the model evaluation kernel has continuous boundary values, controlled differences, and a normalized limit given by an outer factor times a Hardy-space kernel.
- **claim-height-atom** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-height-atom)): For every $\eta>0$ there is $C_\eta<\infty$ such that $$p(H=n)\le C_\eta n^{-7/4+\eta}\qquad(n\ge1).$$
  - Explanation: The probability that one irreducible piece has a given height decays faster than the tail estimate by a full power, up to an arbitrarily small loss.
- **claim-planar-atoms** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-planar-atoms)): There is $C<\infty$ such that, for every integer $k\ge1$, $$\label{c:two-atom-bounds} \sup_w\Pr(S_k=w)\le Ck^{-8/3},\qquad \sum_{m\in\mathbb Z}\sup_{H(w)=m}\Pr(S_k=w) \le Ck^{-4/3}.$$
  - Explanation: Sums of independent pieces satisfy a planar atom bound and a bound on the sum over heights of the heightwise maximal atoms.
- **claim-parallel-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-parallel)): Fix a bound on the ratio of transverse displacement to height. For compatible prescribed ports on two parallel pure cuts at integral height $h\to\infty$, normalize the critical bridge weights by their total mass. For every $\epsilon>0$, $$\Pr\{h^{4/3-\epsilon}\le L \le h^{4/3+\epsilon}\}\longrightarrow1.$$ The convergence is uniform under the fixed displacement bound. It also holds in convex pure-sided domains in a fixed compact family of nondegenerate scaled geometries, with the ports on opposite parallel sides and with order-$h$ clearance from every other side.
  - Explanation: For prescribed ports on two parallel pure cuts with bounded displacement ratio, the walk length concentrates at height to the four thirds, uniformly, and also in convex pure-sided domains with clearance.
- **claim-tilted-law** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-tilted-law)): In a pure wedge of angle $\pi/3$ or $2\pi/3$, take one prescribed port on each boundary ray. Suppose they have order-$R$ clearance to the opposite cut and distance $O(R)$ from the apex. Under the critical chord law their length is $R^{4/3+o(1)}$ in probability. The conclusion persists after restriction to the hexagons of Theorem <a href="#c:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="c:main">1</a> with the same side-interior limiting data.
  - Explanation: In a pure wedge with one prescribed port per ray and order-scale clearances, the walk length is of order scale to the four thirds in probability, also after restriction to the hexagons.
- **claim-arch-mean** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-arch-mean)): For two half-plane boundary ports separated by each integer $g\to\infty$, the probability law $\kappa^{L(\gamma)}/K_g$ satisfies $$L(\gamma)=g^{4/3+o(1)}\quad\hbox{in probability},\qquad \mathbb E L(\gamma)=g^{4/3+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: For each large gap between two half-plane boundary ports, the arch length is of order gap to the four thirds both in probability and in mean.
- **claim-calibrated-inputs** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-calibrated-inputs)): For the port-rooted irreducible law, write $Y,Z,L,D$ for integral height, lateral displacement, length and diameter. Then $2Z$ is integral, $\Pr(D>r)\le Cr^{-3/4}$, $1-\mathbb E e^{-tY}\asymp t^{3/4}$, and $1-\mathbb E e^{-tL}=t^{9/16+o(1)}$. In addition:* 1. *For $k$ independent whole pieces, $\sup_j\Pr(S_Y(k)=j)\le Ck^{-4/3}$ and $\sup_w\Pr(S_{(Y,Z)}(k)=w)\le Ck^{-8/3}$. The off-axis bridge construction gives positive mass in directional windows; all atoms use the actual lattice.* 2. *Start two independent sequences at the same bottom port through the same first center $v$. Retain completed pieces at heights strictly below $s$, excluding the first piece reaching or passing $s$. Fix left and right roles. At every pair of renewal levels $0<d_1<d_2<s$ from the left and right histories respectively, require that the prefixes be disjoint after $v$, leave on opposite sides of $v$, and that every right-prefix crossing at $d_1$ lie strictly right of the left terminal coordinate. The probability of all these requirements is at most $C_\eta(1+s)^{-3/4+\eta}$ for each $\eta>0$. At a shared turn this applies with $s=1$ vacuously, or with $s$ sufficiently below both adjacent arm heights that distant endpoint adjustments do not affect the tests. For the two arms used below, extending both to the same top port duplicates just their highest center. The resulting factor of $\kappa$ is absorbed by an absolute constant; no shape-dependent trimming is used in this application.* 3. *For every sufficiently small fixed $\xi>0$, some $c,C>0$ give the absolute critical mass estimate $$\sum_{\substack{\omega\text{ rooted at a fixed center}\\ R\le\mathop{\mathrm{diam}}\omega\le2R,\ L(\omega)\le R^{4/3-\xi}}} \kappa^{L(\omega)}\le C e^{-R^c}.$$
  - Explanation: Calibrated renewal inputs for the port-rooted irreducible law: tail and Laplace exponents, atom bounds for sums of pieces, a survival bound for two sequences from a shared turn, and a superpolynomially small mass of short long-diameter paths.
- **claim-truncated-reward** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-truncated-reward)): For the strict irreducible law, as $H\to\infty$, $$\mathbb E[L; Y\le 2H]\le H^{d_*-a+o(1)} .$$
  - Explanation: The expected length of an irreducible piece restricted to bounded height is bounded by a power of the height scale.
- **claim-bottom-survival** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-bottom-survival)): Start two independent upward irreducible sequences at bottom ports a distance $g\ge1$ apart, assigning left and right roles by their starts. Let $\Pi_g(S)$ be the probability that their horizontal endpoint order is preserved weakly at every common renewal height strictly below $S$. For $S\ge2$, $$\Pi_g(S)\le C(g/S)^{1/4}\sqrt{\log(2+S)}.$$
  - Explanation: Two independent upward sequences from separated bottom ports keep their horizontal order weakly up to a given height with probability bounded by a power of the ratio of separation to height times a logarithmic factor.
- **claim-confined-arch-mean** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-confined-arch-mean)): Fix a half-plane boundary source and choose a sufficiently large fixed $C$. Give each arch with terminal gap in $[h,2h]$ on one chosen side and diameter at most $Ch$ its critical weight, then normalize over this entire set of terminal ports. For every integer $h\to\infty$, $$L(\gamma)=h^{4/3+o(1)}\quad\hbox{in probability},\qquad \mathbb E L(\gamma)=h^{4/3+o(1)}.$$
  - Explanation: For arches with terminal gap in a macroscopic window and confined diameter, normalized over all terminal ports, length is of order height to the four thirds in probability and in mean.
- **claim-density-one** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-density-one)): There is one set $G\subseteq\mathbb N$ with $\|G\cap[1,N]\|/N\to1$ such that, for every fixed $\delta,\varepsilon>0$, all sufficiently large $h\in G$ satisfy $$h^{4/3-\varepsilon}\le\frac{b_h^{(L)}(e)}{b_h(e)} \le h^{4/3+\varepsilon} \quad(e\in\mathbb Z+h/2,\ \|e\|\le h^{1-\delta}).$$
  - Explanation: On one set of heights of natural density one, the ratio of length-weighted to plain endpoint bridge mass is of order height to the four thirds, uniformly over sublinear offsets.
- **claim-mixture** (theorem, [source](#stmt-c-mixture)): Give each pair $(h,\gamma)$, $h\in E_H$ and $\gamma\in\mathcal B_h(0)$, probability $\kappa^{L(\gamma)}/Z_H$. As $H\to\infty$ through all real scales, $$Z_H=H^{-1/4+o(1)},\quad M_H=H^{13/12+o(1)},\quad \mathbb E_H L=M_H/Z_H=H^{4/3+o(1)}.$$ The height marginal is $b_h(0)/Z_H$.
  - Explanation: In the aligned critical mixture over all even heights in a macroscopic interval, the partition mass, the length-weighted mass and the mean length have the stated power laws.
- **claim-pv-rewards** (bound, [source](#stmt-c-pv-rewards)): Write $J$ for the height of one piece under $i$, and $L$ for its length. Set $\alpha=1/4$, $\beta=3/4$, and $d_*=4/3$. Then $$b=\sum_{k\ge0}i^{*k},\qquad b^{(L)}=b*i^{(L)}*b,$$ $$i(J>r)=r^{-\beta+o(1)},\quad \mathbb E_i[L;J\le r]\le r^{d_*-\beta+o(1)},\quad \mathbb E_i[Le^{-J/r}]=r^{d_*-\beta+o(1)}.$$ The discounted lower reward is retained on pieces with height at most $r\log^2r$ and lateral excursion at most $r\log^4r$.
  - Explanation: Renewal convolution identities for bridge mass and length-weighted bridge mass, with height-tail and reward exponents for the return law used in the mean computation.

Arches of diameter at most $Al$ retain mass $cl^{-5/4}$ for a fixed $A$.*\n\n3.  *From a prescribed port $a$ with inward pure normal $n$, consider the terminal line $m\\cdot(z-a)=s$, where $m$ is its outward pure unit normal and the angle between $n$ and $m$ is at most $\\pi/3$. Here $s>0$ is the Euclidean distance from $a$ to the terminal line. Crossings that stay in the initial half-plane and before the terminal line until their final exit have mass comparable to $s^{-1/4}$. For any fixed positive relative tolerance, and all sufficiently large admissible $s$ with a threshold depending only on that tolerance, the lower bound can be confined within distance equal to that tolerance times $s$ of the segment $a+[0,s]m$. The corresponding two-cut upper bound holds without confinement.*\n\n4.  *In a convex pure domain of diameter $O(R)$, the total length-weighted mass of chords, summed over ordered boundary endpoint pairs, is at most $R^{25/12+o(1)}$.",
```json
      "explanation": "Collects finite estimates taken from a companion work: strip bridge mass decays like a power, length-weighted bridge mass and the arch kernel have matching power bounds, crossing mass to a terminal line is controlled, and total length-weighted chord mass in a convex domain is bounded.",

        "exact_quote": "The mass $p$ is a probability.         "source_id": "PM-237.2"

        "anchor": "stmt-c-mixed-displacement",
        "exact_quote": "For every fixed $C_0<\\infty$ there is $C<\\infty$ such that, for $s\\ge1$, $$\\label{c:mixed-displacement-bound}\n \\sum_{|z|\\le C_0s}D_n(z)(1+|m\\cdot z|)^{-1/4}\\le Cs^{1/2}.$$ If $Y$ is the $m$-projection of one $n$-directed irreducible displacement, then its independent sums also satisfy $$\\label{c:projected-concentration}\n \\sup_y\\Pr\\Bigl\\{\\sum_{j\\le k}Y_j=y\\Bigr\\}\\le Ck^{-4/3},\n \\qquad\n \\sup_w\\mathbb E\\Bigl(1+\\Bigl|w+\\sum_{j\\le k}Y_j\\Bigr|\\Bigr)^{-1/4}\n \\le Ck^{-1/3}.$$",

        "exact_quote": "Let $\\mathcal E$ be an event of input chords from $a$ to the stated terminal interval, of critical mass $E$. The fixed-source attachment construction just described has a translation count $N_s$ on output chords and critical output measure $\\mu_a$ such that $$\\label{c:attachment-moments}\n \\int N_s\\,d\\mu_a\\ge cs^{1/2}E,\n \\qquad\n \\int N_s^2\\,d\\mu_a\\le CsR^{-1/4}.$$ Here the side-1 outer landing is exactly $a$; the side-2 outer landing is free on its support. The estimate applies also to input chords in the full two-cut domain, without a diameter bound.*\n\n\n*If the input chords lie in a convex pure domain of diameter $O(R)$, there is also a construction at scale $s=cR$ with both outer landings free, all outputs in one convex pure domain of diameter $O(R)$, and $$\\label{c:free-attachment-moments}\n \\int N_R^{\\rm free}\\,d\\mu_{\\rm free}\\ge cR^{3/2}E,\n \\qquad\n \\int(N_R^{\\rm free})^2\\,d\\mu_{\\rm free}\\le CR^{7/4}.$$ The measure $\\mu_{\\rm free}$ sums critical weights over ordered output endpoint pairs. In both constructions output length is the input length plus the two attached lengths. Constants are uniform for fixed compact endpoint geometries and independent of the fixed length slack $\\delta$; the required lower threshold for $s$ may depend on $\\delta$.",

      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-c-calibrated-inputs",
        "exact_quote": "For the port-rooted irreducible law, write $Y,Z,L,D$ for integral height, lateral displacement, length and diameter. Then $2Z$ is integral, $\\Pr(D>r)\\le Cr^{-3/4}$, $1-\\mathbb E e^{-tY}\\asymp t^{3/4}$, and $1-\\mathbb E e^{-tL}=t^{9/16+o(1)}$. In addition:*\n\n1.  *For $k$ independent whole pieces, $\\sup_j\\Pr(S_Y(k)=j)\\le Ck^{-4/3}$ and $\\sup_w\\Pr(S_{(Y,Z)}(k)=w)\\le Ck^{-8/3}$. The off-axis bridge construction gives positive mass in directional windows; all atoms use the actual lattice.*\n\n2.  *Start two independent sequences at the same bottom port through the same first center $v$. Retain completed pieces at heights strictly below $s$, excluding the first piece reaching or passing $s$. Fix left and right roles. At every pair of renewal levels $0<d_1<d_2<s$ from the left and right histories respectively, require that the prefixes be disjoint after $v$, leave on opposite sides of $v$, and that every right-prefix crossing at $d_1$ lie strictly right of the left terminal coordinate. The probability of all these requirements is at most $C_\\eta(1+s)^{-3/4+\\eta}$ for each $\\eta>0$. At a shared turn this applies with $s=1$ vacuously, or with $s$ sufficiently below both adjacent arm heights that distant endpoint adjustments do not affect the tests. For the two arms used below, extending both to the same top port duplicates just their highest center. The resulting factor of $\\kappa$ is absorbed by an absolute constant; no shape-dependent trimming is used in this application.*\n\n3.  *For every sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$, some $c,C>0$ give the absolute critical mass estimate $$\\sum_{\\substack{\\omega\\text{ rooted at a fixed center}\\\\\n     R\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\omega\\le2R,\\ L(\\omega)\\le R^{4/3-\\xi}}}\n     \\kappa^{L(\\omega)}\\le C e^{-R^c}.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-237.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-c-calibrated-inputs",
          "exact_quote": "For the port-rooted irreducible law, write $Y,Z,L,D$ for integral height, lateral displacement, length and diameter. Then $2Z$ is integral, $\\Pr(D>r)\\le Cr^{-3/4}$, $1-\\mathbb E e^{-tY}\\asymp t^{3/4}$, and $1-\\mathbb E e^{-tL}=t^{9/16+o(1)}$. In addition:*\n\n1.  *For $k$ independent whole pieces, $\\sup_j\\Pr(S_Y(k)=j)\\le Ck^{-4/3}$ and $\\sup_w\\Pr(S_{(Y,Z)}(k)=w)\\le Ck^{-8/3}$. The off-axis bridge construction gives positive mass in directional windows; all atoms use the actual lattice.*\n\n2.  *Start two independent sequences at the same bottom port through the same first center $v$. Retain completed pieces at heights strictly below $s$, excluding the first piece reaching or passing $s$. Fix left and right roles. At every pair of renewal levels $0<d_1<d_2<s$ from the left and right histories respectively, require that the prefixes be disjoint after $v$, leave on opposite sides of $v$, and that every right-prefix crossing at $d_1$ lie strictly right of the left terminal coordinate. The probability of all these requirements is at most $C_\\eta(1+s)^{-3/4+\\eta}$ for each $\\eta>0$. At a shared turn this applies with $s=1$ vacuously, or with $s$ sufficiently below both adjacent arm heights that distant endpoint adjustments do not affect the tests. For the two arms used below, extending both to the same top port duplicates just their highest center. The resulting factor of $\\kappa$ is absorbed by an absolute constant; no shape-dependent trimming is used in this application.*\n\n3.  *For every sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$, some $c,C>0$ give the absolute critical mass estimate $$\\sum_{\\substack{\\omega\\text{ rooted at a fixed center}\\\\\n     R\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\omega\\le2R,\\ L(\\omega)\\le R^{4/3-\\xi}}}\n     \\kappa^{L(\\omega)}\\le C e^{-R^c}.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-237.2"

      "exact_statement": "For the port-rooted irreducible law, write $Y,Z,L,D$ for integral height, lateral displacement, length and diameter. In addition:*\n\n1.  *For $k$ independent whole pieces, $\\sup_j\\Pr(S_Y(k)=j)\\le Ck^{-4/3}$ and $\\sup_w\\Pr(S_{(Y,Z)}(k)=w)\\le Ck^{-8/3}$. The off-axis bridge construction gives positive mass in directional windows; all atoms use the actual lattice.*\n\n2.  *Start two independent sequences at the same bottom port through the same first center $v$. Retain completed pieces at heights strictly below $s$, excluding the first piece reaching or passing $s$. Fix left and right roles. At every pair of renewal levels $0<d_1<d_2<s$ from the left and right histories respectively, require that the prefixes be disjoint after $v$, leave on opposite sides of $v$, and that every right-prefix crossing at $d_1$ lie strictly right of the left terminal coordinate. The probability of all these requirements is at most $C_\\eta(1+s)^{-3/4+\\eta}$ for each $\\eta>0$. At a shared turn this applies with $s=1$ vacuously, or with $s$ sufficiently below both adjacent arm heights that distant endpoint adjustments do not affect the tests. For the two arms used below, extending both to the same top port duplicates just their highest center. The resulting factor of $\\kappa$ is absorbed by an absolute constant; no shape-dependent trimming is used in this application.*\n\n3.  *For every sufficiently small fixed $\\xi>0$, some $c,C>0$ give the absolute critical mass estimate $$\\sum_{\\substack{\\omega\\text{ rooted at a fixed center}\\\\\n     R\\le\\mathop{\\mathrm{diam}}\\omega\\le2R,\\ L(\\omega)\\le R^{4/3-\\xi}}}\n     \\kappa^{L(\\omega)}\\le C e^{-R^c}.$$",
```

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **port** ([source](#c:introduction)): A *port* is the midpoint of a boundary edge of a domain made of whole triangles.
- **pure cut** ([source](#c:introduction)): A *pure* cut follows one of the three tiling-edge directions; its admissible translates bound whole triangular rows.
- **strict bridge** ([source](#c:inputs)): A strict bridge stays between its boundary cuts.
- **bridge mass B_h** ([source](#c:inputs)): Write $B_h$ for its critical bridge mass from one fixed bottom port, summed over all compatible top ports, and put $B_0=1$.
- **irreducible strict bridge** ([source](#c:inputs)): An irreducible strict bridge has positive integral height and no intermediate singly crossed level.
- **one-piece weight p** ([source](#c:inputs)): Let $p(\gamma)=\kappa^{L(\gamma)}$ on irreducibles from a fixed port.
- **asymptotic power notation** ([source](#c:inputs)): Throughout, $f(R)=R^{a+o(1)}$ means $R^{a-\varepsilon}\le f(R)\le R^{a+\varepsilon}$ for every fixed $\varepsilon>0$ at all sufficiently large scales, with fixed geometric parameters chosen first.
- **translation cost D_n** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For a lattice translation $z$ with $n\cdot z>0$, let $D_n(z)$ be the critical mass of strict $n$-bridges between the prescribed positions separated by $z$.
- **pair-block mass and height** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Let $r$ be the resulting mass on finite pairs of terminating sequences, initially possibly a subprobability, and let $T$ be their common height.
- **block diameter and lateral difference** ([source](#pm-h-17)): Let $D_*$ be the maximum of the diameters of these two complete segments, and let $Y$ be the second segment’s lateral displacement minus the first’s.
- **dyadic exponents** ([source](#c:arch-dyadic-target)): Put $d_*=4/3$ and $a=3/4$.

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-critical-fugacity | critical weight per visited center | = | $(2+\sqrt2)^{-1/2}$ | fixed constant |
| q-row-spacing | physical row spacing | = | $\sqrt3/2$ | fixed constant |
| q-strip-bridge-mass | strip bridge mass | ~ | $(1+h)^{-1/4}$ | all integers h at least zero |
| q-length-weighted-bridge | length-weighted bridge mass | ≤ | $Ch^{13/12}$ | sufficiently large integers h |
| q-arch-kernel | half-plane arch kernel | ~ | $l^{-5/4}$ | integer l at least one |
| q-total-chord-length-mass | total length-weighted chord mass | ≤ | $R^{25/12+o(1)}$ | summed over ordered boundary endpoint pairs |
| q-connector-mass | mass of connecting paths between prescribed ports | ≥ | $cR^{-5/4}$ | uniform on fixed compact sets of scaled configurations |
| q-window-normalizer | normalizer for fixed source and terminal interval | ~ | $R^{-1/4}$ | each fixed source |
| q-pair-block-atom | atoms of summed lateral differences of pair blocks | ≤ | $Cm^{-2}$ | m at least one |
| q-strip-difference | fixed-order differences of strip mass | ≤ | $C_{\ell,\eta}N^{-1/4-\ell+\eta}$ | N at least one |
| q-height-atom | one-piece height atom | ≤ | $C_\eta n^{-7/4+\eta}$ | n at least one |
| q-planar-atom | planar atom of summed pieces | ≤ | $Ck^{-8/3}$ | k at least one |
| q-arch-mean-length | mean arch length | = | $g^{4/3+o(1)}$ | gap g tending to infinity |
| q-bottom-survival-bound | weak-order survival probability | ≤ | $C(g/S)^{1/4}\sqrt{\log(2+S)}$ | S at least two, g at least one |
| q-mixture-partition-mass | mixture partition mass | = | $H^{-1/4+o(1)}$ | H tending to infinity through all real scales |
| q-length-window | upper length bound of the typical window | ≤ | $R^{4/3+\varepsilon}$ | probability tending to one as R grows |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-finite-inputs** [claim](#stmt-c-finite) / [proof](#stmt-c-finite): Finite bridge, arch and length estimates imported from a companion work -> power bounds on bridge and arch masses
- **step-renewal-inputs** [claim](#stmt-c-renewal) / [proof](#stmt-c-renewal): Unique factorization into irreducible pieces and imported renewal estimates -> independent pieces with tail and Laplace exponents
- **step-connectors** [claim](#stmt-c-connectors) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-connectors): Cut-averaging over junction levels with first and second moment and Cauchy–Schwarz -> lower bound on connecting mass
- **step-fixed-height-window** [claim](#stmt-c-fixed-height-window) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-fixed-height-window): Height window for one piece, Fourier atom bound, then count of pieces -> length window for fixed-height bridges
- **step-positive-projection** [claim](#stmt-c-positive-projection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-positive-projection): Turned two-leg construction with averaged cut and second moment -> bridges with positive projections
- **step-mixed-displacement** [claim](#stmt-c-mixed-displacement) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-mixed-displacement): Symmetrization, Fourier atom bound and renewal splitting into halves -> weighted displacement sum bound
- **step-attachment-moments** [claim](#stmt-c-attachment-proposition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-attachment-proposition): Translate input chords, attach bridges, recover decomposition and count translations -> first and second moments of translation count
- **step-window-law** [claim](#stmt-c-window-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-attachment-proposition-2): Second-moment lift of short and long exceptional events against diameter and total chord bounds -> length law for source with terminal interval
- **step-pair-block** [claim](#stmt-c-pair-block-estimates) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-pair-block-estimates): Common renewal transform, off-axis bridge construction and Fourier inversion -> tails and lateral atoms for common blocks
- **step-arch-kernel** [claim](#stmt-c-arch-kernel-regularity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-arch-kernel-regularity): Monotonicity plus injective prefix-and-join inequality excluding sublinear jumps -> ratio of neighboring kernel values tends to one
- **step-same-side-law** [claim](#stmt-c-same-side-law) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-arch-kernel-regularity-2): Good prefixes from the pair process, atom bound for long joining arches, transfer to hexagon -> length law for same-side ports
- **step-model-kernel** [claim](#stmt-c-model-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-model-kernel): Gram matrix differences, localized inverse, Toeplitz limit and outer factor -> kernel difference bounds and limit
- **step-strip-smoothness** [claim](#stmt-c-strip-smoothness) / [proof](#pm-h-30): Residual estimates at two evaluation points and uniform nonzero denominator in the quotient formula -> difference bounds for strip mass
- **step-height-atom** [claim](#stmt-c-height-atom) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-height-atom): Angular derivative bounds of the generating function and two integrations by parts -> one-piece height atom bound
- **step-planar-atoms** [claim](#stmt-c-planar-atoms) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-planar-atoms): Directional window, Fourier inversion and shell-wise block splitting -> planar and heightwise atom bounds
- **step-parallel-law** [claim](#stmt-c-parallel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-parallel): Discard few and many pieces, then separate-batch bounds on the length tails -> length law for parallel cuts
- **step-tilted-law** [claim](#stmt-c-tilted-law) / [proof](#pm-h-39): Prefix exhaustion, local visit estimate, exterior-arc comparison and removal of exceptional mass -> length law for nonparallel cuts
- **step-main-theorem** [claim](#stmt-c-main) / [proof](#pm-h-39): Case split of the two supports into identical, parallel or nonparallel -> prescribed-endpoint length law
- **step-calibrated-inputs** [claim](#stmt-c-calibrated-inputs) / [proof](#stmt-c-calibrated-inputs): Imported calibrated finite estimates and renewal consequences -> inputs for the mean-length argument
- **step-truncated-reward** [claim](#stmt-c-truncated-reward) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-truncated-reward): Insert a marked piece between two bridges and use the convex-box length bound -> truncated expected piece length
- **step-bottom-survival** [claim](#stmt-c-bottom-survival) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-bottom-survival): Killed transverse walk, near-minimum counting factorization and occupation bound -> weak-order survival bound
- **step-arch-mean** [claim](#stmt-c-arch-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-bottom-survival-2): Marked-arm decomposition allocating independent groups of pieces to survival tests, reward and match -> mean and probability length law for arches
- **step-confined-arch-mean** [claim](#stmt-c-confined-arch-mean) / [proof](#proof-stmt-c-confined-arch-mean): Translate the source, use the convex-domain length bound and divide by the normalizer -> mean law for confined terminal-summed arches
- **step-pv-rewards** [claim](#stmt-c-pv-rewards) / [proof](#stmt-c-pv-rewards): Imported exact reward input and convolution identities -> reward exponents for return law
- **step-density-one** [claim](#stmt-c-density-one) / [proof](#pm-h-51): Endpoint variation bound, pointwise lower bounds and deletion of heights with large suprema -> density-one set of good heights
- **step-mixture** [claim](#stmt-c-mixture) / [proof](#pm-h-51): Sum the pointwise lower bounds and dyadic upper bounds over even heights -> mixture mass and mean laws

```json
      "output": "mixture mass and mean laws",
      "proof_anchor": "pm-h-51"
    }
  ]
}
```
<!-- PM:END pm.roadmap.v2 -->

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-prescribed-endpoint-length-law**: For prescribed macroscopically separated boundary ports, the critical chord length lies in the window from R to the power 4/3 minus epsilon up to R to the power 4/3 plus epsilon with probability tending to one, for every positive epsilon.
- **inv-connector-normalizer**: The critical mass of connecting paths between two macroscopically separated side-interior ports is bounded below by a constant times R to the power minus 5/4, which supplies the normalizer for the prescribed-endpoint law.
- **inv-density-one-mean-ratio**: On one set of heights of natural density one, the ratio of length-weighted bridge mass to bridge mass at a sublinear offset lies between h to the power 4/3 minus epsilon and h to the power 4/3 plus epsilon for all sufficiently large heights in that set.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-arbitrary-height-mean**: Claiming the point-to-point mean-length ratio at an arbitrarily chosen single strip height. -> The statement is proved only for heights in one density-one set; heights outside it are not controlled, so the claimed ratio can fail there.
- **anti-ports-near-apex**: Applying the wedge result to ports that are pinned near the apex or lack macroscopic clearance from the opposite cut. -> The estimates are uniform only on compact families of nondegenerate scaled geometries, so degenerate placement is not covered.
- **anti-probability-implies-mean**: Deducing a mean-length law from the convergence-in-probability length law. -> Convergence in probability does not control the weighted upper tail, so the mean could differ.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The results are asymptotic statements about critical self-avoiding walk measures as the scale tends to infinity, with existential constants and unspecified o(1) rates (for example 'there is $C<\infty$' and 'sufficiently large'); the paper states no finite procedure with explicit steps, so no finite computation can decide any claim.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves asymptotic length laws with existential constants and gives no procedure that a program could execute, so there is no software artifact to build. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Only limit statements in probability are proved; they give no step budgets or simulation rules, and the uniformity claims are restricted to nondegenerate scaled geometries. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The theorems are scaling statements about one lattice model and supply no reusable tool interface or checkable output. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The arguments are probabilistic and analytic: they use renewal decompositions, second-moment bounds and contour identities, none of which is a finite procedure with a stated cost. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The independent irreducible pieces are a probabilistic decomposition of bridges, not a storage layout with defined operations or space bounds. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No computation is specified, so there is nothing to map to hardware; the diameter-confined mass bound is purely mathematical. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Self-avoiding walk weights are a statistical-mechanics object; the paper makes no hardness, randomness or security claim. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | There is no learning problem, dataset or estimator; the pair-block law is a renewal object whose probability statement is not a model. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper studies typical length under a fixed weight, not an extremal or optimized path; its constants may depend on parameters, so no objective can be bounded quantitatively. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, agents or consensus appear; the height mixture is a probability measure on paths, not a protocol. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The natural home is mathematical physics of critical lattice paths; applicability elsewhere is blocked because the density-one result does not hold at every individual height. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-density-one-heights** (hypothesis): The endpoint mean-length ratio at a prescribed top port is proved only for heights in one set of natural density one, with offsets bounded by a sublinear power of the height; heights outside that set are not controlled. Blocked use: ['Asserting the endpoint mean length at an arbitrarily chosen single strip height', 'Using the ratio for offsets comparable to the full height']
- **limit-macroscopic-separation** (hypothesis): The prescribed-endpoint length law requires ports whose scaled limits are distinct points in the relative interiors of sides; the constants are uniform only on fixed compact sets of nondegenerate scaled geometries. Blocked use: ['Applying the law to ports at unit-scale separation', 'Applying the law to ports pinned at corners or lacking macroscopic clearance']
- **limit-wedge-clearance** (edge_case): In the wedge, the result needs one prescribed port on each ray, with order-R clearance to the opposite cut and distance O(R) from the apex; the opening angle is restricted to two values. Blocked use: ['Using the wedge result for ports near the apex', 'Using it for other wedge angles or for parallel-ray configurations']
- **limit-probability-not-mean** (approximation): Convergence in probability of the length does not control the weighted upper tail; the mean statement is proved only for half-plane arches and for the stated terminal-summed or mixture settings. Blocked use: ['Deducing the mean length of prescribed-endpoint walks in hexagons from the probability law', 'Transferring the half-plane mean law to strips or wedges']
- **limit-nonconstructive-constants** (nonconstructive): All bounds carry existential constants and unspecified o(1) rates, for example the lower bound on connecting mass with an unspecified constant c; no explicit finite procedure or numerical threshold is given. Blocked use: ['Using the exponents as hard step budgets at finite size', 'Deriving explicit numerical scale thresholds']
- **limit-critical-honeycomb-weight** (formal_scope): The argument is set on the regular honeycomb lattice with the critical weight per visited center; it relies on the local contour identity with that weight and does not apply to other lattices or other activities. Blocked use: ['Applying the exponent to square or other lattices', 'Applying the law to non-critical weights']
- **limit-imported-finite-inputs** (hypothesis): The finite bridge, arch and renewal estimates are taken from companion works and recorded as inputs; the results here are conditional on them in the sense that their proofs are not reproduced. Blocked use: ['Treating the finite estimates as proved in this paper', 'Using the results without the port convention of the inputs']
- **limit-mixture-aligned** (counterexample_scope): The mixture mean law concerns aligned bridges at even heights in a macroscopic interval with the critical mixture weight; it is not a pointwise statement at a single height. Blocked use: ['Reading the mixture mean as an individual-height law', 'Using it for odd heights or non-aligned endpoints']

### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 237 (`lean/docs/237.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [Polynomial vacuum representations and bridge mass for honeycomb walks](../../preprints/Polynomial-vacuum-representations-and-bridge-mass-for-honeycomb-walks-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Renewal and changes of law for critical honeycomb walks](../../preprints/Renewal-and-changes-of-law-for-critical-honeycomb-walks-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Critical strip-crossing mass on the honeycomb lattice](../../preprints/Critical-strip-crossing-mass-on-the-honeycomb-lattice-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
> - [Critical honeycomb chords with prescribed boundary endpoints](../../preprints/Critical-honeycomb-chords-with-prescribed-boundary-endpoints-September-26-2026/main.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The linked formalization establishes finiteness of the critical bridge measures used in the paper. For every strip height $h\ge1$, the total weight and first length moment of self-avoiding honeycomb bridges from a fixed initial port to a free terminal port are finite. The corresponding sums are also finite when the bridge is confined to the corridor of horizontal width $h(\log h)^2$, including the normalized first-moment sums.
>
> These are supporting summability statements. The paper's $h^{4/3+o(1)}$ mean-length law and its central-visit and hexagonal-chord exponents are outside them.
>
> The linked formalization proves existence of the infinite-length free energy for the critical honeycomb self-avoiding-walk partition function. For every starting vertex, force direction, and real force parameter, the logarithm of the length-$n$ partition function divided by $n$ converges to its stated free-energy limit.
>
> This selected result supplies the limiting free energy. It does not state the paper's small-force exponent, near-critical correlation scale, or the $3/4$ spatial and moment laws.
>
> For critical self-avoiding walks on the honeycomb lattice, the formalization proves that the total weight of paths crossing a strip of height $N$ is comparable to $N^{-1/4}$, while the first horizontal-displacement moment of return paths is comparable to $N^{3/4}$. It also proves finiteness of the strip sums, the exact arch–bridge balance identity, comparison of successive moment increments with bridge mass, and monotonicity of bridge mass. The comparison constants are uniform in the strip height.
>
> For the prescribed-boundary-endpoint companion, let $B_h$ be the total critical weight of strict bridges crossing a strip of integral height $h$, from one fixed bottom port to all compatible top ports, with $B_0=1$. The formalization proves
> $c(1+h)^{-1/4}\le B_h\le C(1+h)^{-1/4}$
> for all $h\ge0$ and fixed positive constants $c,C$. This includes finiteness of the path sum. The prescribed-endpoint length law, arch and strip mean laws, and remaining moment and renewal conclusions are outside this supporting statement.

Comparator source `HoneycombBridgeMassSupport.lean` (2773 bytes, sha256 `50cc48019b0335fda0ba6f79ca1ebe0ba8f0118eed15565c8f80416ccdf43cdc`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace CriticalHoneycomb
open scoped ENNReal

abbrev Center := ℤ × ℤ × Bool

def up (i j : ℤ) : Center := (i, j, true)
def down (i j : ℤ) : Center := (i, j, false)

/-- Adjacency across a full triangular edge. -/
def Adj (v w : Center) : Prop :=
  if v.2.2 then
    w.2.2 = false ∧ ((w.1 = v.1 ∧ w.2.1 = v.2.1) ∨
      (w.1 = v.1 ∧ w.2.1 = v.2.1 - 1) ∨
      (w.1 = v.1 - 1 ∧ w.2.1 = v.2.1))
  else
    w.2.2 = true ∧ ((w.1 = v.1 ∧ w.2.1 = v.2.1) ∨
      (w.1 = v.1 ∧ w.2.1 = v.2.1 + 1) ∨
      (w.1 = v.1 + 1 ∧ w.2.1 = v.2.1))

noncomputable def rowSpacing : ℝ := Real.sqrt 3 / 2

/-- The fixed Euclidean embedding in the unit triangular tiling. -/
noncomputable def centerPosition (v : Center) : ℝ × ℝ :=
  if v.2.2 then
    ((v.1 : ℝ) + (v.2.1 : ℝ) / 2 + 1 / 2,
      rowSpacing * ((v.2.1 : ℝ) + 1 / 3))
  else
    ((v.1 : ℝ) + (v.2.1 : ℝ) / 2 + 1,
      rowSpacing * ((v.2.1 : ℝ) + 2 / 3))

/-- A midpoint port, oriented into the triangle domain. -/
structure Port (D : Set Center) where
  inside : Center
  outside : Center
  adjacent : Adj inside outside
  inside_mem : inside ∈ D
  outside_not_mem : outside ∉ D

noncomputable def Port.position {D : Set Center} (p : Port D) : ℝ × ℝ :=
  ((centerPosition p.inside).1 / 2 + (centerPosition p.outside).1 / 2,
    (centerPosition p.inside).2 / 2 + (centerPosition p.outside).2 / 2)

/-- A simple center trace, with no premature boundary exit. -/
def IsTrace (D : Set Center) (p : List Center) : Prop :=
  p.Nodup ∧ p.IsChain Adj ∧ ∀ v ∈ p, v ∈ D

noncomputable def kappa : ℝ := (2 + Real.sqrt 2) ^ (-(1 : ℝ) / 2)

/-- Length counts centers; the port half-edges contribute no factors. -/
noncomputable def weight (p : List Center) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  ENNReal.ofReal (kappa ^ p.length)

/-- The source's strip and tilt-compatible terminal support. -/
def strip (h : ℕ) : Set Center := {v | 0 ≤ v.2.1 ∧ v.2.1 < (h : ℤ)}
def StrictBridge (h : ℕ) :=
  {p : List Center // IsTrace (strip h) p ∧ p.head? = some (up 0 0) ∧
    ∃ i : ℤ, p.getLast? = some (down i ((h : ℤ) - 1))}
noncomputable def bridgeMass (h : ℕ) : ℝ≥0∞ :=
  if h = 0 then 1 else ∑' p : StrictBridge h, weight p.val

/-- Necessary estimate c:finite(1), inputs.tex:13, on the actual path sum. -/
def FiniteBridgeMassEstimate : Prop :=
  ∃ c C : ℝ, 0 < c ∧ 0 < C ∧ ∀ h : ℕ,
    ENNReal.ofReal (c * (1 + (h : ℝ)) ^ (-(1 : ℝ) / 4)) ≤ bridgeMass h ∧
    bridgeMass h ≤ ENNReal.ofReal (C * (1 + (h : ℝ)) ^ (-(1 : ℝ) / 4))

/-- Trusted reference hole only; missing necessary main obligation. -/
theorem finiteBridgeMassEstimate : FiniteBridgeMassEstimate := by
  sorry

end CriticalHoneycomb


end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


Critical self-avoiding walks between prescribed, macroscopically separated boundary ports of a regular honeycomb hexagon have length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$ in probability, where <i>R</i> is the scale of the hexagon. We prove the corresponding statements for half-plane arches, parallel cuts and nonparallel pure cuts. The half-plane law also has mean length $`R^{4/3+o(1)}`$. A separate strip argument gives endpoint mean laws on one density-one set of heights and in an aligned, critically weighted mixture of all even heights in a macroscopic interval.
